Oh, gosh... um. I really try not to be rude, I promise. I know how important it is to let people finish, especially at work. But... well, sometimes, yes.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... gosh. Well, to be perfectly honest, this is actually sort of the main reason I decided to come in and see you. I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, because I really do pride myself on doing a good job, but... yes. I've been having a really hard time keeping my mind on a task long enough to actually finish it anymore. Even when I know it's incredibly important. 

Like, um... I'll sit down to trace a client's network problem, and I'll be trying so hard to just get it done. But then ten minutes will go by, and I'll suddenly realize I've just reread the exact same log entries three times in a row, and I still have no idea what they actually say. 

It's so frustrating, and I feel terrible about it. I really don't want my boss or the clients to think I'm slacking off or incompetent, because I promise I'm trying my absolute best to just finish my work. Is that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

And I still have absolutely no idea what they actually say. \n\nIs that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say anxious. I don't think so, anyway. I just kind of drift off, I guess? It's definitely not a panic thing or feeling overwhelmed by the difficulty of the task. I just sort of lose the thread. I hope that makes sense. Oh, um... always? Gosh, I don't want to make it sound like I was a bad kid or anything, because I really did try to be good! But, well... I suppose it's not entirely new. 

I mean, my mother... she actually still jokes about it sometimes. She says that back in grade school, my report cards would always say I was a bright boy, which was nice of the teachers to say! But, um... they also said I never stayed in my seat, and I guess I had a bad habit of blurting out answers before I was called on. I just really hope I haven't been too much of a bother to people because of it. Is that the kind of history you needed to know? I hope I'm giving you the right details.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... changes? Well... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I always want to be someone people can rely on. I try so hard to be dependable, I really do. But... yes, things have changed quite a bit lately. 

I used to have a very structured job, and that seemed to help keep me on track. But since I lost that structure... well, I've had to try and manage things on my own with freelance work, and I'm afraid it hasn't gone as smoothly as I'd hoped. I'm so embarrassed to say this, but my reputation has taken a bit of a hit. I've actually lost a few freelance clients because... well, because I couldn't seem to get the work done when I said I would. 

And, um, there was a senior service-manager role I was really hoping for, but I missed out on it. I think people are just having a hard time trusting me to follow through on things right now, which is just an awful feeling. I never want to let anyone down. It's even gotten to the point where my wife and I have had to worry about money a little bit, which makes me feel terrible as a husband. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds really bad. I promise I'm not trying to make excuses, and I'm really motivated to get back on track! I just... I want to be completely honest with you so you can help me. Did I explain that alright? I hope I'm not coming across as irresponsible.

Well, um... I guess, like I was just saying with my mom's stories, those little quirks have probably been around since I was a boy. But I always tried so hard to keep it all together, you know? I really did my best to compensate so it wouldn't affect anyone else.

Like I'm being a distraction. I always stop right away when they do, I promise! I really don't want to bother anyone, and it's just a nervous habit, really. \n\nIs that... does that count as fidgeting? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Did that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um. I really try not to be rude, I promise. I know how important it is to let people finish, especially at work. But... well, sometimes, yes. \n\nIt happens a lot when we're having tech discussions, or when a user is explaining an issue to me. The answer just sort of... jumps right into my head, you know? And before I can stop myself, I'm already talking over them, or... or even reaching over to grab their keyboard to just fix it while they're still talking. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, and I apologize immediately. I don't mean to be disrespectful or make them feel unheard. It's just that the solution is right there, and it just sort of bursts out of me. \n\nDoes that... is that the kind of thing you're asking about? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible person to work with. I'm trying to get better at it, I really am. I mean, I don't go out and buy sports cars on a whim or anything crazy like that! Maybe I'll occasionally start a new project at home when I should be doing something else, but... I think everybody does that sometimes, don't they? \n\nI really haven't noticed anything major. I hope that's the right kind of answer. If there's something specific you mean, I can try to think harder on it! I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you leave tasks unfinished, even when you know you need to complete them?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh. Oh, um... no, I've never been diagnosed with anything like that. And I've certainly never taken any medication for it! 

I mean, I've always just tried to work hard and push through things on my own, you know? I wouldn't want to take up a doctor's time with something I should be able to manage myself. I could be looking at a support ticket at 9 AM or 3 PM and it's just the same sort of drifting. I really wish I could give you a better pattern to work with. Am I supposed to be tracking that? I can definitely start keeping a log if that would make things easier for you to figure out! I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. Did that answer the question alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, maybe a little? I mean, I try really hard not to be disruptive or anything. But I guess... well, if I'm sitting with a client, or, um, I go to these chess meetings, and I'll suddenly realize my leg is just bouncing away. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or... twisting a little plastic cable tie around in my fingers. \n\nI usually don't even notice I'm doing it until somebody finally gives me that... that look, you know? Like I'm being a distraction. I always stop right away when they do, I promise! I really don't want to bother anyone, and it's just a nervous habit, really. \n\nIs that... does that count as fidgeting? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Did that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um. I really try not to be rude, I promise. I know how important it is to let people finish, especially at work. But... well, sometimes, yes. \n\nIt happens a lot when we're having tech discussions, or when a user is explaining an issue to me. The answer just sort of... jumps right into my head, you know? And before I can stop myself, I'm already talking over them, or... or even reaching over to grab their keyboard to just fix it while they're still talking. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, and I apologize immediately. I don't mean to be disrespectful or make them feel unheard. It's just that the solution is right there, and it just sort of bursts out of me. \n\nDoes that... is that the kind of thing you're asking about? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible person to work with. I'm trying to get better at it, I really am. Was that a helpful answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're not talking to someone, like doing something sudden without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... impulse? Like, doing things without thinking... gosh, I hope I don't do that too much. I try really hard to be deliberate about things, especially at my age. You'd think I'd have all that sorted out by now, right? \n\nBut, um... when I'm just on my own? I don't know, I guess I'm pretty normal in that regard. I mean, I don't go out and buy sports cars on a whim or anything crazy like that! Maybe I'll occasionally start a new project at home when I should be doing something else, but... I think everybody does that sometimes, don't they? \n\nI really haven't noticed anything major. I hope that's the right kind of answer. If there's something specific you mean, I can try to think harder on it! I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you leave tasks unfinished, even when you know you need to complete them?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh. Well, to be perfectly honest, this is actually sort of the main reason I decided to come in and see you. I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, because I really do pride myself on doing a good job, but... yes. I've been having a really hard time keeping my mind on a task long enough to actually finish it anymore. Even when I know it's incredibly important. \n\nLike, um... I'll sit down to trace a client's network problem, and I'll be trying so hard to just get it done. But then ten minutes will go by, and I'll suddenly realize I've just reread the exact same log entries three times in a row, and I still have no idea what they actually say. \n\nIt's so frustrating, and I feel terrible about it. I really don't want my boss or the clients to think I'm slacking off or incompetent, because I promise I'm trying my absolute best to just finish my work. Is that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel emotionally overwhelmed or have intense mood swings?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... emotionally overwhelmed? Gosh, I really try not to let things get to me. I mean, I like to think of myself as a pretty even-keeled guy, especially at work. You kind of have to be when you're dealing with frustrated people and broken computers all day, right? \n\nI really don't think I have intense mood swings or anything like that. I mean, sure, I get a little stressed sometimes when the ticket queue gets backed up, but... I don't go flying off the handle or breaking down crying or anything dramatic. I try really hard to keep a positive attitude and just push through. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like I'm hiding anything! I just really haven't noticed my emotions being a big problem. I suppose it's not entirely new. \n\nI mean, my mother... she actually still jokes about it sometimes. She says that back in grade school, my report cards would always say I was a bright boy, which was nice of the teachers to say! But, um... they also said I never stayed in my seat, and I guess I had a bad habit of blurting out answers before I was called on. Oh, and I apparently lost my homework a lot, even when I had actually done it. \n\nI'm a little embarrassed to admit all that, honestly. I'd like to think I've matured a lot since then! But I guess... yeah, I suppose the trouble with focusing and being a bit fidgety has kind of always been there in the background. I just really hope I haven't been too much of a bother to people because of it. Is that the kind of history you needed to know? I hope I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to make any significant changes to your daily routine or work habits because of these focusing and fidgeting issues?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... changes? Well... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I always want to be someone people can rely on. I try so hard to be dependable, I really do. But... yes, things have changed quite a bit lately. \n\nI used to have a very structured job, and that seemed to help keep me on track. But since I lost that structure... well, I've had to try and manage things on my own with freelance work, and I'm afraid it hasn't gone as smoothly as I'd hoped. I'm so embarrassed to say this, but my reputation has taken a bit of a hit. I've actually lost a few freelance clients because... well, because I couldn't seem to get the work done when I said I would. \n\nAnd, um, there was a senior service-manager role I was really hoping for, but I missed out on it. I think people are just having a hard time trusting me to follow through on things right now, which is just an awful feeling. I never want to let anyone down. I promise I'm not trying to make excuses, and I'm really motivated to get back on track! I just... I want to be completely honest with you so you can help me. Did I explain that alright? Oh, um... well, I suppose sometimes I do have a little trouble with that. I mean, I really try my best to be respectful and attentive, especially when someone is talking to me or when I'm in a meeting. I know how important it is to be a good listener!

I really don't want to be a burden or rely on pills if I don't absolutely have to. \n\nI hope it's okay that I haven't seen anyone about this before now. I just always thought it was a personal failing I needed to work on, rather than something medical. Does that seem reasonable? I mean, I really try my best to be respectful and attentive, especially when someone is talking to me or when I'm in a meeting. I know how important it is to be a good listener! \n\nBut, gosh, it's hard to explain without sounding like I'm just being rude or impatient, which I promise I'm not trying to be! It's almost like a physical urge where I feel like I just need to stand up, or maybe check something on my computer, or just move on to the next problem right that very second. \n\nI really try to keep it hidden, though! I'll tap my foot quietly or just grip my pen a little tighter so I don't disrupt anyone. I hope that doesn't make me sound too difficult to be around. Do you think that's the kind of restlessness you were asking about? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. But I guess... yeah, I suppose the trouble with focusing and being a bit fidgety has kind of always been there in the background. I just really hope I haven't been too much of a bother to people because of it. Is that the kind of history you needed to know? I hope I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to make any significant changes to your daily routine or work habits because of these focusing and fidgeting issues?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... changes? But... yes, things have changed quite a bit lately. \n\nI used to have a very structured job, and that seemed to help keep me on track. But since I lost that structure... well, I've had to try and manage things on my own with freelance work, and I'm afraid it hasn't gone as smoothly as I'd hoped. I know how important it is to let people finish, especially at work. But... well, sometimes, yes. \n\nIt happens a lot when we're having tech discussions, or when a user is explaining an issue to me. The answer just sort of... jumps right into my head, you know? And before I can stop myself, I'm already talking over them, or... or even reaching over to grab their keyboard to just fix it while they're still talking. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, and I apologize immediately. I don't mean to be disrespectful or make them feel unheard. It's just that the solution is right there, and it just sort of bursts out of me. \n\nDoes that... is that the kind of thing you're asking about? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible person to work with. I'm trying to get better at it, I really am. Was that a helpful answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're not talking to someone, like doing something sudden without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... impulse? Like, doing things without thinking... gosh, I hope I don't do that too much. I try really hard to be deliberate about things, especially at my age. You'd think I'd have all that sorted out by now, right? \n\nBut, um... when I'm just on my own? I don't know, I guess I'm pretty normal in that regard. I mean, I don't go out and buy sports cars on a whim or anything crazy like that! Maybe I'll occasionally start a new project at home when I should be doing something else, but... I think everybody does that sometimes, don't they? \n\nI really haven't noticed anything major. I hope that's the right kind of answer. If there's something specific you mean, I can try to think harder on it! I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you leave tasks unfinished, even when you know you need to complete them?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh. Well, to be perfectly honest, this is actually sort of the main reason I decided to come in and see you. I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, because I really do pride myself on doing a good job, but... yes. I've been having a really hard time keeping my mind on a task long enough to actually finish it anymore. Even when I know it's incredibly important. \n\nLike, um... I'll sit down to trace a client's network problem, and I'll be trying so hard to just get it done. But then ten minutes will go by, and I'll suddenly realize I've just reread the exact same log entries three times in a row, and I still have no idea what they actually say. \n\nIt's so frustrating, and I feel terrible about it. I really don't want my boss or the clients to think I'm slacking off or incompetent, because I promise I'm trying my absolute best to just finish my work. Is that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel emotionally overwhelmed or have intense mood swings?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... emotionally overwhelmed? Gosh, I really try not to let things get to me. I mean, I like to think of myself as a pretty even-keeled guy, especially at work. You kind of have to be when you're dealing with frustrated people and broken computers all day, right? \n\nI really don't think I have intense mood swings or anything like that. I mean, sure, I get a little stressed sometimes when the ticket queue gets backed up, but... I don't go flying off the handle or breaking down crying or anything dramatic. I try really hard to keep a positive attitude and just push through. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like I'm hiding anything! I just really haven't noticed my emotions being a big problem. I want to be as helpful as possible, though—is there a specific kind of reaction you're looking for? I can try to think of an example if that would help you more. Did I answer that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble focusing and sitting still, or is this something that has developed more recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... always? Gosh, I don't want to make it sound like I was a bad kid or anything, because I really did try to be good! But, well... I suppose it's not entirely new. \n\nI mean, my mother... she actually still jokes about it sometimes. She says that back in grade school, my report cards would always say I was a bright boy, which was nice of the teachers to say! But, um... they also said I never stayed in my seat, and I guess I had a bad habit of blurting out answers before I was called on. Oh, and I apparently lost my homework a lot, even when I had actually done it. \n\nI'm a little embarrassed to admit all that, honestly. I'd like to think I've matured a lot since then! But I guess... yeah, I suppose the trouble with focusing and being a bit fidgety has kind of always been there in the background. I just really hope I haven't been too much of a bother to people because of it. Is that the kind of history you needed to know? I hope I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to make any significant changes to your daily routine or work habits because of these focusing and fidgeting issues?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... changes? But... yes, things have changed quite a bit lately. \n\nI used to have a very structured job, and that seemed to help keep me on track. But since I lost that structure... well, I've had to try and manage things on my own with freelance work, and I'm afraid it hasn't gone as smoothly as I'd hoped. I'm so embarrassed to say this, but my reputation has taken a bit of a hit. I've actually lost a few freelance clients because... well, because I couldn't seem to get the work done when I said I would. \n\nAnd, um, there was a senior service-manager role I was really hoping for, but I missed out on it. I think people are just having a hard time trusting me to follow through on things right now, which is just an awful feeling. I never want to let anyone down. It's even gotten to the point where my wife and I have had to worry about money a little bit, which makes me feel terrible as a husband. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds really bad. I promise I'm not trying to make excuses, and I'm really motivated to get back on track! I just... I want to be completely honest with you so you can help me. Did I explain that alright? I hope I'm not coming across as irresponsible.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming extremely focused on a task, to the point where you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... extremely focused? I wouldn't want to ignore anyone or make them feel like they aren't important! \n\nI suppose there are times when I'm working on a really tricky computer issue and I get, you know, engaged in trying to fix it.
